### Q14. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

Let a line with direction ratios <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></math> be perpendicular to the lines with direction ratios <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math>. If the point of intersection of the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math> and the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></math> is equal to ________.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given a line with direction ratios <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></math> be perpendicular to the lines with direction ratios <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math>. If the point of intersection of the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math> and the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></math> is equal to ________.</p><p>Now given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8869;</mo></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>14</mn><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8943;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>Also <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8869;</mo></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>14</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8943;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>From Equations <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced></math> we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>16</mn><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>14</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>So, line becomes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math></p><p>Let points be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math></p><p>As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> satisfies plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let the lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>18</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>28</mn></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac></math> be coplanar and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> be the plane containing these two lines. Then which of the following points does NOT lies on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>18</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>28</mn></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac></math> are coplanar</p><p>Now using the condition of coplanarity we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mn>27</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>20</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>31</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>&#955;</mi></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>&#955;</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>Now, normal of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>, which contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>11</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Now equation of required plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> will be</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>11</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>11</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now satisfy all given points from option we get, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> does not lie on plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

Let the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> intersect the plane containing the lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8477;</mi></math> at the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>. Then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></math> equals ______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **12**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given plane contain line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>So equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> will satisfy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>16</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>8</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8943;</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Now normal vector of the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and direction ratio along the line which contained the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>j</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi></math></p><p>Since they will be perpendicular,</p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>11</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8943;</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Now on solving equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math> we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>So, now equation of plane will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now let the point in the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>t</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>7</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> satisfy the equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>So <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q17. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

The plane passing through the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>l</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and perpendicular to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi></math> is the acute angle between the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> and the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>-axis, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>415</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>cos</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>&#952;</mi></math> is equal to ______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **125**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given the plane passing through the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub><mo>:</mo><mi>l</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and perpendicular to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>,</p><p>So direction ratio of line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>l</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Now direction ratio of line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>l</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Also plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math> will contain the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>So, normal of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math> will be perpendicular to the line</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>l</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>So, direction ratio of line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now finding angle with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math> axis</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>49</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>83</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>415</mn><msup><mi>cos</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>83</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>415</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>125</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q18. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

<p>The largest value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math>, for which the perpendicular distance of the plane containing the lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>, is ______. </p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Direction ratio of plane containing these lines is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>a</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Also plane is passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, equation of plane will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now distance of point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> from this plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><msqrt><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#183;</mo><msqrt><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> largest value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q19. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> be the plane containing the straight line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>9</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> and perpendicular to the plane containing the straight lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi></math> is the distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>11</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>147</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>96</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>32</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>54</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>32</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>&#62;</mo></math> be direction ratios of plane containing the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Since plane normal will be perpendicular to given lines then by condition of perpendicular lines, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math> </p><p>On solving <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math> we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mrow><mn>24</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>35</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>b</mi><mrow><mn>15</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>16</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mrow><mn>14</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>So, DR of plane are<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>11</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#62;</mo></math></p><p>Now let DR of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo></math></p><p>Then, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>11</mn><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>4</mn></mfenced></math> (as plane normal will be perpendicular to given line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>9</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>)</p><p>Now solving equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mn>4</mn></mfenced></math> we get, direction ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, equation of plane will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>21</mn></math></p><p>Now finding distance of point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>11</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> from plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>21</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>22</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>21</mn></mrow><msqrt><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>32</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q20. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> lie on the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>q</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math>, for some <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi></math>. The shortest distance of  the plane from the origin is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>109</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>142</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>71</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>142</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>142</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>So, the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> will satisfy the given plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>q</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math> ... (i)</p><p>Also given line is perpendicular to normal plane so </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>q</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> ... (ii)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>q</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>22</mn></math></p><p>So,equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>22</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now its distance from origin <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>6</mn><msqrt><mn>710</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>142</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

### Q21. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>:</mo><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>d</mi></math> contain the line of intersection of two planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn></math>. If the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> passes through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>, then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mn>13</mn><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math> is equal to

- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>95</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>97</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>93</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let the equation of plane passing through the line of intersection of planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>i.e. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Since it passes through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Hence, equation of plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>13</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>64</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>93</mn><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>13</mn></math></p><p>Value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>13</mn><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>93</mn></math></p>

</details>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac></math>

- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>11</mn></mfrac></math>

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mi>L</mi><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>L</mi><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></math> are coplanar then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mtable><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math Now, normal vector will be, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>9</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, equation of plane is given by, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now finding perpendicular distance from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p>so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>b</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>c</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>i.e. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Let the vector parallel to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>b</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>b</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Equation of line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Let point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is lying on plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math>, so, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
So, point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p>

</details>

plane</p><p>Equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></math></p><p>The direction ratio of the line are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>The line is in the plane</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn></math></p>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo></math> Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math> is point of intersection of line and plane</p><p>So it should satisfy the given plane.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Square of distance from point <math mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math ### Q26. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The distance of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> from the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math> measured parallel to a line, whose direction ratios are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></math>, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math>,</p><p>Point is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>And the direction ratio of plane parallel to the given plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p>Therefore, Equation of plane when D.R of parallel plane is given is,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>since, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>11</mn></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Distance between points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#38;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>11</mn></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mtext>&#160;unit</mtext></math></p>

</details>

### Q27. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> be the foot of the perpendicular from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>)</mo></math> on the plane containing the lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>
Then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo></math> is equal to ______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **96**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Equation of the plane</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"></math>By cross multiplication method, we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>A</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>B</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi ### Q29. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

Let a plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> pass through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and contain the line, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>.</mo></math> If distance of the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> from the origin is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo></math> then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> is equal to

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The required plane passes through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Assume, the equation of the plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>The line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math> lies on the required plane.</p><p>So, we can say that </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Use cross-multiplication to solve the equations <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>b</mi><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>14</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>b</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>14</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>14</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math>  (Assume)</p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math></p><p>Put the values of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi></math> in the required equation of plane we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>So, the distance of the plane from the origin is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mn>3</mn></mfenced><msqrt><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> </p>

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

If the lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math> are co-planar, then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi></math> is _______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math> are co-planar.</p><p>Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>4</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>6</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q31. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let the foot of perpendicular from a point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math> to the straight line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi></math>. Let a line be drawn from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> parallel to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> which meets <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi></math> is the acute angle between the lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>N</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#945;</mi></math> is equal to <u>             </u>.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Assume, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></math></p><p>So, ### Q32. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> be a plane containing the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> and parallel to the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>. If the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>)</mo></math> lies on the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>, then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is equal to ___ .

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **38**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Equation of plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>4</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>4</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>7</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>)</mo></math>  lies on it so</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>5</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>4</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>4</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>7</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>38</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>38</mn></math></p>

</details>
