### Q55. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the line, <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> lies in the plane <math><mi>l</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>,</mo></math> then <math><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mn>5</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math > <mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow>  <mn>26</mn> </mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math > <mrow>  <mn>18</mn> </mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math > <mn>2</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> lies in <math><mi>l</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn></math>

The point <math> <mrow> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>4</mn> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow> </mrow> </math> lies on the plane. So it should satisfy the equation of the plane.

<math><mn>3</mn><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn></math>

<math><mn>3</mn><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math> .......(i)

<math><mo>∴</mo></math> Line lies in plane

<math><mo>∴</mo></math> line is perpendicular to the normal to the plane

<math><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mn>2</mn><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> .......(ii)

<math><mi>l</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo></math> <math><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2015 (04 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The distance of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> from the point of intersection of the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>12</mn></mfrac></math> and the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=16</mo></math>, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <msqrt> <mn>1</mn> <mn>4</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mn>8</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> <mn>1</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>12</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>t</mi></math>  

General points is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

It lies on the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

Hence, the point of intersection will be

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>14</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

Distance from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>4</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </msqrt> </mrow> </math></p><p><math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <msup> <mn>4</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mn>3</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mn>1</mn> <msup> <mn>2</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </msqrt> </mrow> </math></p><p><math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </math></p>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2014 (19 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the angle between the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></math> and the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mi>&#955;</mi></msqrt><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>&#960;</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>, then the value of <math> <mrow> <mi>&#955;</mi> </mrow> </math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>3</mn> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>3</mn> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given line is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> and plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">:</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mi>&#955;</mi></msqrt><mo>z</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>The angle between a line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mi>l</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mi>n</mi></mfrac></math> and a plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>n</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><msqrt><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><msqrt><msup><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>


         <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/02f75c1a-9aea-4f2c-8c04-c29708af034e-140.png" style="width: 326px; height: 183px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>6</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mi>&#955;</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfenced><mrow><msqrt><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><msqrt><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mi>&#955;</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfenced><mrow><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></msqrt><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mi>&#955;</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mi>&#955;</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></msqrt></math></p><p>Squaring both sides, we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>1</mn><mn>6</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>16</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q58. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The image of the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> in the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+3=0</mo></math> is the line

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We first find the intersection of line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></math> with the plane. Any general point on line can be written as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>.</p><p>Substituting in equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
Which gives <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> which is not possible.</p><p>So, we conclude that the line is parallel to the plane.<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8757;</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math> The reflected line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></math> will also have the same direction ratios. i.e.,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>.</mo></math><be>
Consider the normal perpendicular to the plane and line.
Its direction ratios are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math></be></p><p><be><math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> <mspace></mspace>&#160; </mrow> </math> Equation of normal is</be></p><p><be><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math></be></p><p><be><math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> <mspace></mspace> </mrow> </math> Any general point can be written as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math> substituting in the equation of a plane,</be></p><p><be> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></be></p><p><be><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>r</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></be></p><p><be><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>r</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></be></p><p><be><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></math></be>

<u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/EM0025803_Quest_1.jpg" style="width: 230px; height: 192px;" />​</strong></u>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> <mspace></mspace> </mrow> </math> The point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is the midpoint of segment <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>C</mi></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> <mspace></mspace>&#160; </mrow> </math>Equation of line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>y</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>z</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math></p>

</details>

### Q59. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $A(-3,2)$ and $B(-2,1)$ be the vertices of a triangle $\mathrm{ABC}$. If the centroid of this triangle lies on the line $3 x+4 y+2=0$, then the vertex $C$ lies on the line :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4 x+3 y+5=0$
- **(B)** $3 x+4 y+3=0$
- **(C)** $4 x+3 y+3=0$
- **(D)** $3 x+4 y+5=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $3 x+4 y+3=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\mathrm{C}=\left(x_1, y_1\right)$
$$
\mathrm{A}(-3,2)
$$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/qEa6UjFUKrQAArbX4sA8PR4lHPLLAsDoNjZyb6BUsQA.original.fullsize.png">

Centroid, $\mathrm{E}=\left(\frac{x_1-5}{3}, \frac{y_1+3}{3}\right)$
Since centroid lies on the line
$$
\begin{aligned}
& 3 x+4 y+2=0 \\
\therefore \quad & 3\left(\frac{x_1-5}{3}\right)+4\left(\frac{y_1+3}{3}\right)+2=0
\end{aligned}
$$
$$
\Rightarrow \quad 3 x_1+4 y_1+3=0
$$
Hence vertex $\left(x_1, y_1\right)$ lies on the line
$$
3 x+4 y+3=0
$$

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2013 (07 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>k</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math> are coplanar, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi></math> can have

**Options:**

- **(A)** exactly two values.
- **(B)** exactly three values.
- **(C)** any value.
- **(D)** exactly one value.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) exactly two values.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></math>

and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math> to be coplanar if 

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. </p><p>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>k</mi></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>, &#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></math>

Hence, there are exactly two values of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q61. JEE Main 2012 (26 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The equation of a plane containing the line $\frac{x+1}{-3}=\frac{y-3}{2}=\frac{z+2}{1}$ and the point $(0,7,-7)$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+y+z=0$
- **(B)** $x+2 y+z=21$
- **(C)** $3 x-2 y+5 z+35=0$
- **(D)** $3 x+2 y+5 z+21=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x+y+z=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The equation of the plane containing the line $\frac{x+1}{-3}=\frac{y-3}{2}=\frac{z+2}{1}$ is a $(x+1)+b$ $(y-3)+c(z+2)=0$ where $-3 a+2 b+c=0$
This passes through $(0,7,-7)$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore a(0+1)+b(7-3)+c(-7+2)=0 \\
& \Rightarrow a+4 b-5 c=0 \quad \text {...(B) }
\end{aligned}
$$
On solving equation (A) and (B) we get $a=1, b=1, c=1$
$\therefore$ Required plane is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& x+1+y-3+z+2=0 \\
& \Rightarrow x+y+z=0
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q62. JEE Main 2012 (19 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\vec{a}=\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k}, \vec{b}=2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}-\hat{k}$ and $\vec{c}=r \hat{i}+\hat{j}+(2 r-1 \hat{k}$ are three vectors such that $\vec{c}$ is parallel to the plane of $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$, then $r$ is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** $-1$
- **(C)** 0
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 0**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\vec{a}=\hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k}$, and
$$
\vec{c}=r \hat{i}+\hat{j}+(2 r-1) \hat{k}
$$
Since, $\vec{c}$ is parallel to the plane of $\vec{a}$ and $\vec{b}$ therefore, $\vec{a}, \vec{b}$ and $\vec{c}$ are coplanar.
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
1 & -2 & 3 \\
2 & 3 & -1 \\
r & 1 & 2 r-1
\end{array}\right|=0 \\
& \Rightarrow 1(6 r-3+1)+2(4 r-2+r)+ \\
& \Rightarrow 6 r-2+10 r-4+6-9 r=0 \\
& \Rightarrow r=0
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q63. JEE Main 2012 (07 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A line with positive direction cosines passes through the point $P(2,-1,2)$ and makes equal angles with the coordinate axes. If the line meets the plane $2 x+y+z=9$ at point $Q$, then the length $P Q$ equals

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{2}$
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** $\sqrt{3}$
- **(D)** 1

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\sqrt{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Point $P$ is $(2,-1,2)$
Let this line meet at $Q(h, k, w)$
Direction ratio of this line is
$(h-2, k+1, w-2)$
Since, $d c_s$ are equal $\& d r_s$ are also equal,
So, $h-2=k+1+w-2$
$\Rightarrow \quad k=h-3$ and $w=h$
This line meets the plane
$2 x+y+z=9$ at $Q$, so,
$2 h+k+w=9$ or $2 h+h-3+h=9$
$\Rightarrow 4 h-3=9 \Rightarrow h=3$
and $k=0$ and $w=3$
Distance
$$
\begin{aligned}
P Q & =\sqrt{\left(3-2^2\right)+\left(0-(-1)^2\right)+(3-2)^2} \\
& =\sqrt{1^2+1^2+1^2}=\sqrt{3}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q64. JEE Main 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the angle between the line $x=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z-3}{\lambda}$ and the plane $x+2 y+3 z=4$ is $\cos ^{-1}\left(\sqrt{\frac{5}{14}}\right)$, then $\lambda$ equals

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3}{2}$
- **(B)** $\frac{2}{5}$
- **(C)** $\frac{5}{3}$
- **(D)** $\frac{2}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{2}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\cos \theta=\sqrt{\frac{5}{14}}$
$\sin \theta=\frac{3}{\sqrt{14}}$
$\sin \theta=\frac{1+4+3 \lambda}{\sqrt{1+4+\lambda^2} \sqrt{1+4+9}}$
$\frac{3}{\sqrt{14}}=\frac{5+3 \lambda}{\sqrt{5+\lambda^2} \sqrt{14}} \Rightarrow \lambda=\frac{2}{3}$

</details>

### Q65. JEE Main 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let the line $\frac{x-2}{3}=\frac{y-1}{-5}=\frac{z+2}{2}$ lies in the plane $x+3 y-\alpha z+\beta=0$. Then $(\alpha, \beta)$ equals

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(6,-17)$
- **(B)** $(-6,7)$
- **(C)** $(5,-15)$
- **(D)** $(-5,15)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $(-6,7)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Dr's of line $=(3,-5,2)$
Dr's of normal to the plane $=(1,3,-\alpha)$
Line is perpendicular to normal $\Rightarrow 3(1)-5(3)+2(-\alpha)=0 \Rightarrow 3-15-2 \alpha=0 \Rightarrow 2 \alpha=-12 \Rightarrow \alpha=-6$
Also $(2,1,-2)$ lies on the plane
$$
\begin{aligned}
& 2+3+6(-2)+\beta=0 \Rightarrow \beta=7 \\
& \therefore(\alpha, \beta)=(-6,7)
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q66. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If $(2,3,5)$ is one end of a diameter of the sphere $x^2+y^2+z^2-6 x-12 y-2 z+20=0$, then the coordinates of the other end of the diameter are

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(4,9,-3)$
- **(B)** $(4,-3,3)$
- **(C)** $(4,3,5)$
- **(D)** $(4,3,-3)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $(4,9,-3)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Coordinates of centre $(3,6,1)$
Let the coordinates of the other end of diameter are $(\alpha, \beta, \gamma)$ 
then $\frac{\alpha+2}{2}=3, \frac{\beta+3}{2}=6, \frac{\gamma+5}{2}=1$
Hence $\alpha=4, \beta=9$ and $\gamma=-3$.

</details>

### Q67. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The distance between the line $\vec{r}=2 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k}+\lambda(\hat{i}-\hat{j}+4 \hat{k})$ and the plane $\vec{r} \cdot(\hat{i}+5 \hat{j}+\hat{k})=5$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{10}{9}$
- **(B)** $\frac{10}{3 \sqrt{3}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{3}{10}$
- **(D)** $\frac{10}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{10}{3 \sqrt{3}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Distance between the line
$\vec{r}=2 \hat{i}-2 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k}+\lambda(\hat{i}-\hat{j}+4 \hat{k})$ and the plane $\vec{r} \cdot(\hat{i}+5 \hat{j}+\hat{k})=5$ is
equation of plane is $x+5 y+z=5$
$\therefore$ Distance of line from this plane
= perpendicular distance of point $(2,-2,3)$ from the plane
i.e. $\left|\frac{2-10+3-5}{\sqrt{1+5^2+1}}\right|=\frac{10}{3 \sqrt{3}}$.

</details>

### Q68. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If the plane $2 a x-3 a y+4 a z+6=0$ passes through the midpoint of the line joining the centres of the spheres
$$
x^2+y^2+z^2+6 x-8 y-2 z=13 \text { and }
$$
$x^2+y^2+z^2-10 x+4 y-2 z=8$, then a equals

**Options:**

- **(A)** -1
- **(B)** 1
- **(C)** -2
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) -2**

**Detailed Solution:**

Plane
$2 a x-3 a y+4 a z+6=0$ passes through the mid point of the centre of spheres $x^2+y^2+z^2+6 x-8 y-2 z=13$ and $x^2+y^2+z^2-10 x+4 y-2 z=8$ respectively centre of spheres are $(-3,4,1) \&(5,-2,1)$
Mid point of centre is $(1,1,1)$
Satisfying this in the equation of plane, we get
$2 a-3 a+4 a+6=0 \Rightarrow a=-2$

</details>

### Q69. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the angle $\theta$ between the line $\frac{x+1}{1}=\frac{y-1}{2}=\frac{z-2}{2}$ and the plane $2 x-y+\sqrt{\lambda} z+4=$ 0 is such that $\sin \theta=\frac{1}{3}$ the value of $\lambda$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{5}{3}$
- **(B)** $\frac{-3}{5}$
- **(C)** $\frac{3}{4}$
- **(D)** $\frac{-4}{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{5}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Angle between line and normal to plane is
$\cos \left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta\right)=\frac{2-2+2 \sqrt{\lambda}}{3 \times \sqrt{5+\lambda}}$ where $\theta$ is angle between line \& plane
$$
\Rightarrow \sin \theta=\frac{2 \sqrt{\lambda}}{3 \sqrt{5+\lambda}}=\frac{1}{3}
$$
$\Rightarrow \lambda=\frac{5}{3}$.

</details>

### Q70. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The intersection of the spheres $x^2+y^2+z^2+7 x-2 y-z=13$ and $x^2+y^2+z^2-3 x+3 y+4 z=8$ is the same as the intersection of one of the sphere and the plane

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x-y-z=1$
- **(B)** $x-2 y-z=1$
- **(C)** $x-y-2 z=1$
- **(D)** $2 x-y-z=1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2 x-y-z=1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Required plane is $S_1-S_2=0$ where $S_1=x^2+y^2+z^2+7 x-2 y-z-13=0$ and
$$
\begin{aligned}
& S_2=x^2+y^2+z^2-3 x+3 y+4 z-8=0 \\
& \Rightarrow 2 x-y-z=1
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q71. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The radius of the circle in which the sphere
$x^2+y^2+z^2+2 x-2 y-4 z-19=0$ is cut by the plane $x+2 y+2 z+7=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 4
- **(B)** 1
- **(C)** 2
- **(D)** 3

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/T0BYWjJWjCUXBRGX-nssQYBFulSITFkm-xFfjUhjIJY.original.fullsize.png">
 centre of sphere $=(-1,1,2)$
Radius of sphere $\sqrt{1+1+4+19}=5$
Perpendicular distance from centre to the plane
$\mathrm{OC}=\mathrm{d}=\left|\frac{-1+2+4+7}{\sqrt{1+4+4}}\right|=\frac{12}{3}=4$
$\mathrm{AC}^2=\mathrm{AO}^2-\mathrm{OC}^2=5^2-4^2=9 \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}=3$

</details>
